not useful. Tops that keep coming apart or skirts that keep
riding up or splits that won’t stay closed are not useful
clothes. When the Blackman sees the Blackwoman wearing
an outfit she is visibly uncomfortable in he should request
that she change into something more comfortable and more
suitable for a respectful woman. If she is dealing in a circle
where fine clothes are the prerequisite for acceptance she
should be encouraged to get out of it.
Chapter 12
HER RELIGION
This is a sticky topic and one that must be approached
with great care. No matter what the affiliation, the
Blackwoman is dedicated to her religion (if she has one).
She will honor and respect her church as if God himself
resides there. She donates or tithes a lot of her salary and
personal funds to her church. She joins church clubs,
choirs, boards and groups and incorporates them into her
life. She works tirelessly for causes related to her house of
worship. If she attends church regularly the Blackman may
notice that some of the outfits she wears to church are
inappropriate, too tight, too short or too low cut. Her church
is another place she goes to see who can dress the best.
While the Blackman does not approve of the outfits she
wears to church he is usually not in a position to protest
effectively. It is also common knowledge that the church is
not immune to scandal and many of the membership have
clandestine illicit relationships going on among the so-called
holy. There is always a lot of gossip going around about
who did what to whom, or who caught who with whom or
who’s sneaking around behind another spouses back. The
Blackman quickly loses interest in going to church because
his street experience allows him to cut through the religious
rap and recognize the money game that’s often going on.
He sometimes recognizes the preacher as a bible-toting
holy rolling pimp of sorts who milks the female members
out of big dough. The hypocrisy and fakery repels him. The
Blackwoman respects her pastor as her perceived link to
God Almighty and she serves him, raises money for him,
cooks for him and follows his instructions some times to the
letter. She often treats her minister like she ought to treat her
man. It is trying for the Blackman to watch his woman work
so diligently for another Blackman when he can’t get any
cooperation from her at home.
If the Blackman does not attend church as regularly as
the Blackwoman or refuses to go at all, the Blackwoman
tends to feel sorry for him and believes that his salvation is at
stake. She thinks that if he went to church more his luck
might improve. There are always more Blackwomen in
church than Blackmen. This is not because Blackwomen are
trying harder to get to glory, but because the Blackman is nat
prone to sit up and listen for hours to another Blackman tell
him how to run his life. He does not like it when his woman confesses their private affairs to the minister and holds his decisions and recommendations more dear than his. Blackwomen obey their pastors because his instructions do not require her to have to deal with him in follow-up. She has options and the pastor appreciates any efforts she makes to aid him in his program. She doesn’t have to live with him
122
daily so her reverence is reserved for Sunday, or choir
rehearsal or usher board meeting. The Blackwoman views
church as a permissible place to let her hair down and
confess her sins publicly. She believes that her private
relationship with God is separate from everything else she
does and she claims to turn to God to help her problems with
the Blackman. She sincerely says: “I put it in the hands of
the Lord.” Often she belongs to a quiet dignified religion that
does not require an outcry.
Another shocker is when the Blackwoman gets in church
and works herself into a frenzy commonly called “shouting.”
This is a process during which she voluntarily loses control
of her bodily limbs, screams, cries loudly and becomes
nearly hysterical due to being overcome with religious
worship. She calls it “feeling the spirit.” She flings her
arms, throws up her dress and kicks her legs wildly while
tossing her head from side to side. She is not embarrassed
about this kind of public performance and the other
Blackwomen there feel good about the fact that she got
“happy.” Many time male ushers rush to her side and
physically carry her from the pew because she is disruptive
and possibly dangerous to other members sitting near who
may get hit or kicked while she is out of her head. She has
this fit to let off her frustration in a place where this kind of
tantrum is permitted based on religion. It has been said that
the “shouting” phenomena is a frenzy during which the
person actually ends up with a feeling similar to an orgasm.
The Blackman is embarrassed about the whole ordeal and
does not believe that God requires the Blackwoman to have
an epileptic type seizure to profess her belief. And he is
right. It’s all a game.
The Blackwoman will cling to her Bible for comfort and
read it in the Blackman’s presence. She may sit around the
house humming spirituals, singing aloud appealing to God
for mercy in her affairs. It gives her a feeling of calm. Of
course she dismisses the parts in the Bible that refer to what
her role should be as a woman. She picks and chooses
which parts of the Bible she will believe and reduces the rest
to old time information that doesn’t apply today. She ignores
these parts and decides for herself what God wants her to be
and do. She believes that God or Jesus said such-in-such
during the olden days which do not apply to modern times.
123
She believes herself to be very righteous due to attending
and being involved in the church. She feels that if she can be
busy with other activities during the week that she can at
least give one day to God - and attend church services. She
should give all of her days to God, not just Sunday, and she
should discuss her concepts of God and her practice of
religion with her man. She has a continuing need for
approval by her church members and often counts on them
for her self-esteem. Her self-esteem is closely related to the
perception of esteem by her peers and nameless others which
she perpetually tries to attain. On the other hand the
Blackman is considered a misfit because he rejects the role
demands placed on him by others. He is his own man. If he
does things in a way that is contrary to the way suggested by
common opinion he is called wrong. If he persists in his
own ideas and refuses to change them he is called stubborn.
He is known to execute his will in the face of countless
odds.
The Blackwoman, not understanding the purpose of
religion, goes to church to worship a God of some sort who
is a member of the masculine entity. He is a man. He is built
like a man. He is made like a man, and is referred to as “He”
or “Him” or “His.” God, the supreme being, has always
been a man. His masculine gender has never been doubted
or tampered with. He has always been and always will be a
man. The Blackwoman accepts the actual fact that God is
indeed a male. She reads and hears about the thoughts and
wishes of God and is respectful to his words, professes to
love him, fears him and memorizes his instructions. She
periodically even tries to obey God and do what he says to
reach heaven (happiness) here and in the other life she
believes is waiting for her in glory after she dies.
She can honor the God/man in this way because she
knows that it is highly unlikely she will ever meet the
supreme God/man face to face, or have to answer his
questions, or be held personally accountable for her actions
under direct examination. The supreme being, the creator,
can be a man to her as long as the God/man does not live with her. It is simple for her to claim she wants to obey God because it is not her man she is talking about. If the
Blackwoman had to live with God, since he is a man also, she could not get along with him either. She does not
124
understand that her behavior towards a man will and does
not change, no matter which man it is. Since the Blackman is
in the image of the God/man she should also respect, honor
and obey him too. Her man may be the closest symbol she
ever meets who resembles the God. But she likes to think
that all of this is just a fluke of nature. The point that her
Blackmate is a man, and God is a man, does not phase her.
The similarity shoots right over her holy head.
It should be explained to her that the two major
differences between the Black/man and the God/man are
power and knowledge. She should be told that:
POWER is the ability to cause others to adjust their
behavior in conformance with the instructions given.
Power can be wielded multifarious ways to get the
desired result. Reduced to its lowest common
denominator power is simply strength. Proven real
or perceived by demonstration.
KNOWLEDGE is the acquaintance with facts, truths
and principles. Comprehension, judgement and
wisdom go with the territory. It is an absolute
knowing of information.
The object that prevents the Black/man from acquiring
the power and knowledge to be in line behind the God/man
is the Blackwoman. She is a wall between the Blackman and
his developing a certain measure of the kind of power and
knowledge it takes to rule, to rule the world and to rule her.
She does not obey him or tell him the truths that would allow
him to change her behavior - so he does not have power or
knowledge. She keeps him from attaining full knowledge
because she will not tell. She blocks him from attaining full
knowledge because she will not tell. She blocks him from
being acquainted with her true-self and doesn’t let him know
very much about what she knows or believes. She does not
assist him in his creations and is short on patience. If he tries
something and fails once, that’s it. And she never lets him
forget it.
Her lack of support of him in his multifaceted quests and
her denial of the moral approbation to execute his own ideas
keeps him from succeeding. He is like a man in a sinking
boat full of people with buckets and the only one bailing
water is him. If the boat sinks he will be charged with the
failure to preserve the occupant’s lives. If he succeeds in
125
saving them, he is told it was what he was supposed to do
anyway. He seemingly has white America holding him
down on one end and the Blackwoman putting him down on
the other. If he were not so strong and self-motivated he
would have been destroyed centuries ago.
While keeping the Blackman from getting to power and
knowledge, she does something else completely juxtaposed
to that position. She expects him to have the answer to all of
her problems. Often the Blackman will report to each other
about the Blackwoman and say: “she got too many
problems.” As soon as the Blackwoman is sure she has the
ear of the Blackman she starts to complain and drags out all
of her psychological hang-ups for him to sift through, solve
and make better. This is not a bad idea for her to do this
except that she usually has no intention of taking his advice.
She asks him anyway to tear apart his theories. Yet she
looks to him as a mystic who will decipher her problems of
adjustment and make it all better. She may be attracted to
Buddhism or witchcraft or even atheism trying to find her
own personal God. Sometimes the Blackwoman turns to her
Zodiac sign as religiously as she turns to her church. Zodiac
books, a familiar kind of numerology, based on the sun,
moon, stars, planets, and months, are sold every where and
purchased by those desiring to know the future. The
Blackwoman will pattern and temper her behavior sometimes
according to her daily zodiac reading. She chooses
numerology, mythology and tricknology before she will take
her daily instructions from her man. She likes to obey a
“thing” as opposed to obeying a live man. She wants to obey
and follow an entity that cannot monitor her or communicate
normally with words. She insists upon having some kind of
magic governing her life. She believes in a ridiculous
assortment of misinformation she receives or buys from
people whom she does not know. She moves to yoga,
crystals or meditation, seeking a God she can design ‘an
control. To know avail. In the rare cases when she does
listen to the Blackman, after he drags her to the answers to her problems, she looks up one day and decides that she did it all be herself. All alone. She develops amnesia about his input and guidance, claims that God showed her the way, and may decide she no longer needs the man. She may want to shake him off because she doesn’t want anyone present to
126
remind her of her lame past of failure and frustration. She
wants to impress the other people around her with her selfdirected, self-achieved success. The history of her botching
up every thing she tried before she got to her Blackman is
conveniently dismissed from memory. She does not want to
be beholden.
This is particularly true of a Blackwoman with mediocre
intelligence whose man helps her to get through school or
training and to get on their feet professionally or personally.
It may involved showing her how to establish credit, drive
or buy a car, dress appropriately or speak right. These are
special categories that the Blackwoman wants to believe she
accomplished alone from her own inspiration and
knowledge. Once on her feet and making a little money she
immediately computes how she can exist on her own without
having to put up with her man. He becomes instantly
dispensable. She is willing to dispense with the vessel that
rendered to her all her blessings. Earning a little money
makes the Blackwoman feel independent and free. She may
have no idea what kind of life she will live without the
Blackman, but her fantasies tell her that it can always be
better - if she is available. Almost the very second she
achieves her goals she decides she is superior to her man.
After that point, she may just tolerate him until she can
actually figure out the logistics of getting away. She prays to
God. Sadly, she believes that the God/man will support her
in fighting against the Black/man, who is God’s own kind -
another man. Because she equates the God/man with mind
boggling wondrous miracles she cannot see him in her own
Black/man. But he is there. He is only repressed. It is this
kind of reality that the Blackwoman finds hard to handle.
She finds this troublesome to accept because she does not
see herself as being worthy of associating with a being who
is in the image of God. A man. The Blackwoman’s gross
national product regarding religion is confusion. She has not
yet been able to conceptualize God. Some of them do not
instill a religion in their children and say they will let the
child, who is unqualified to make this decision, decide for
itself what kind of religion it wants to have. Children should
be taught the religion of their parents, and their parents
should have the same religion.
Chapter 13 EDUCATION AND CAREER
The so-called professional Blackwoman is a wonder to
behold. She loves her job and the public acclaim it affords
her. She is a workaholic when challenged by her boss to
complete certain tasks. She tries to make comrades of her coworkers and often confides in them about her home life and
will try to build her entire life around her job - if allowed to.
Her main focus is to achieve the highest possible title
available in her field and to make the most money doing so.
She also hopes to be considered the best dressed employee
ever. She will spend humongous sums of money on clothes
so she can look more prosperous than she is. Somewhere
along the line the Blackwoman adopted the foolish idea that
she should wear a different outfit to work everyday - of the
year if possible. She is so selfish and self centered she
believes that her co-workers count her outfits and by doing
so can tell what she has, so she puts forth a great effort to
make sure they think she has a ton of clothes.
While on her tip-top job she stays late for meetings,
volunteers for special assignments, hauls home a briefcase
full of papers every night, and will treat her boss (white or
Black) with extra charm if she thinks it will help her get
ahead in both title and salary. She is exceptionally proud of
her alma mater and prides herself on getting a full college
education that qualifies her for special jobs. She thinks it
makes her superior, and if her man happens not to have
attended college she will throw it up in his face whenever
she has a sophisticated talk with him about something he’s
not familiar with. She learned a lot of her nonsense about the
Blackwoman’s place from her formal education or should it.
be called mis-education? She is seen to adjust her values an
demeanor to fit it and get along on her job. All of the on-thejob principles that white Americans adhere to are accepted by
her and she tries to live up to them also. If her man tells her she is spending too much time on her job she may rebel and say he is just jealous of her career and doesn’t want her to get ahead and make more money than he does. She has the
same kind of irrational explanation for everything’ the
128
Blackman asks of her while she is working on her big-time
gig. She will come home bragging about her
accomplishments on the job or about her great boss and what
a fantastic man he is, or that she just spent such-n-such on a
present for her boss, or “my boss in taking me out to
dinner.” It matters not to her that her own family or man
needs her at home to fix dinner for them. She convinces the
Blackman that she can’t be stopped because she is on a roll
moving up in the world. A world that is not hers because her
world is one that should be rotating around the Black nation.
Unlike any other species the Black female rejects her natural
mate and offspring to cling to the culture of others in the
name of holding down a job. She reminds the Blackman of
his perceived financial shortcomings by saying “you know
we need the money.” If she gets a hard earned promotion
she boasts to everyone in earshot about how she pleased her
employer enough with her dedicated service to get promoted.
She doesn’t know that the Blackman doesn’t mind her
earning a high salary, it doesn’t really make him feel less of
a man and he doesn’t really feel a need to compete with her,
it’s just that she can’t handle it herself. Her feelings of guilt
are self-nurtured. She is the one who becomes
uncomfortable about how much money she earns especially
if it’s more than her man. If she shared her money with her
man and put it all in one pot she would not be so apt to feel
superior or to make him feel inferior. Nevertheless she
parades around claiming that she wishes she could find a
man who is not intimidated by her high salary and corporate
image. Her equation that money equals power in her
relationship with the Blackman is unfounded yet she thinks
money is the measure of who is in charge of making
decisions in the home. Of course this idea creates a lot of
problems for the Blackman in the home. The Blackman is
supposed to be the boss in his home and it has nothing to do
with who makes the most money. The Blackwoman belongs
to the Blackman and every thing she owns also belongs to
him. All ancient Black civilizations were set up in this way
because it is a system that allows the man to be in a more
powerful position with the added clout of the wealth of his
wives. If a Blackwoman nags the Blackman about not
making enough money or expounds daily about how he
should have more ambition, he will surely seek out another
129
mate who accepts him without consulting his wallet. The
Blackwoman mistakenly thinks that if she nags the
Blackman long enough or stays mad at him long enough, or
refuses his affections long enough, that it will make him give
in and submit to her ideas. Instead, it runs him away and he
is outside looking for peace of mind. This peace of mind
may come in the form of another woman, drugs, alcohol or
other than normal perversions - of which there are plenty.
When the Blackwoman is cross with the Blackman and
breaks his peace he is unable to function in his normal way.
If he is able to function it is because he has been forced to
develop an “I don't care” or “F-it” attitude that restores him
to calm. He develops this attitude because of the futility of
trying to get his points over to the Blackwoman who rejects
them. She wants him to suffer if she is displeased with him
in any way. She will withhold the peace until she is satisfied
he has been punished enough and has learned his lesson.
Well, not exactly his lesson, but her lesson. She fights in the
same way to defend her right to work, control “her own”
money and to be excused from womanly duties.
If she attends a party at work she often uses another
voice, laughs at jokes or incidents which are not funny to
Blacks, and is chummy with her co-workers. Although she
is not on the job to socialize or fraternize she becomes adept
at appearing to be interested in office jokes, sometimes off
color stories, and other kinds of conversations not fit for the
ears of a Blackwoman when told by strangers. On some jobs
the Blackwoman is still subject to be pinched, patted on the
behind, touched in a familiar way and fondled at will by her
“boss” or “bosses.”
The Blackwoman sometimes accepts this kind of
disrespectful action because she wants to keep her job and
thinks that if she does not go along with the touching and
patting that she will be fired. And she will in most cases»
Most of this goes on on several levels of employment. On
the more classy jobs many of them file law suits claiming
sexual harassment, but on more menial jobs Blackwomen do not know how to defend themselves. If the Blackman should get wind of a report the Blackwoman is being offended physically by her boss he should demand that she quit the job. No job is so important that the Blackwoman exchanges her dignity for a few dollars more.
130
Older Blackwomen are also subject to this kind of
behavior from their male bosses and feel they are defenseless
because they need the job. Blackmen should question their
women, their sisters and their mothers to make sure they are
not being disrespected on the job.
Unfortunately, interracial dating has become common
place in the workplace and many Blackwomen feel honored
if their white boss or white co-worker shows special
personal interest in them. Office romances of all kinds are
apt to surface if she is spending several hours a day with
another man other than her own. If a man and woman spend
extensive time together and develop intimate communications
from the close proximity they are working in daily, or
nightly, the Blackman should be aware that nature will take
its course. Unless the two workers are totally repulsed by
each other the potential for an affair is present. The
Blackwoman will work closely and dedicated with the men
on her job and cooperate fully to complete a project. Of
course her motivation is financial - it’s her job she thinks she
is performing just for the money. If she must work outside
the home, and many Blackwomen have talents that others
can benefit from, the Blackman should make sure that the
environment is safe. Safe from sexual harassment and safe
from overly close involvement with other men. The
Blackwoman romanticizes a lot and she is not above
comparing her boss or co-worker to her man at home and
deciding that the former has more to offer her or that they
have more in common. Today’s ideas of work are specific
and competitive and those who have chosen to work must
adopt the same ideas of the job hosting them. The
Blackwoman is not exempt from these perspectives. She has
leapt into the fray head first. Things are seemingly moving
so fast she no longer has time to examine her motivations or
actions. She looks about her and sees what’s going on with
others and believes she must match the tempo of their pace if
she wants to achieve. She is constantly concerned about
what “they” will think about how she dresses, how she
looks, what she has, and who her man is. She notes that the
Blackman is more often than not absent, missing from the
job scene because of lack of skills, prison, old age, drugs,
craziness, illness and dissatisfaction. She feels more alone
today than she has ever been before. The media have set up
131
such a bad and unwholesome picture of the Blackman that
she is often embarrassed to even be associated with a
Blackman who is not wearing a Suit or carrying a briefcase.
She considers it beneath her dignity to develop a relationship
with what is referred to as a “blue collar worker”. She has
adopted the standards of white America and believes her
education makes her superior to the Blackman who may not
have the same interests or options. In fact, if a so-called
intelligent, attractive, successfully employed Blackwoman
dates or becomes genuinely interested in a Blackman who
her friends consider to be below her social status, her friends
will criticize her and remind her that she should be with a
doctor or lawyer, etc.
Blackwoman think that they are too good for some
Blackmen when in effect it is the direct opposite, some
Blackmen are too good to have to put up with some of the
funky dispositions of today’s Blackwomen. The Blackman
should be himself and exercise his power and will over the
Blackwoman. Her Harvard degree or office door name plate
has no place in her personal affairs with her man. It has no
value there. The only place her training or education has any
value is on her job. And she should be reminded to leave her
hoity-toity behavior on the job where it belongs. This is not
to say that the Blackwoman should be kept down on the
farm rooting potatoes, certainly she should be educated - in
intelligence, courteousness, cleanliness, cheerfulness and
dependability.
The Blackwoman is equally baffled about why the
unemployed Blackman cannot find a job and settle into it for
a zillion years until retirement. It appears so simple to her
and she has no problem finding work of some kind if she
really sets her mind to it. She has several survival cushions
at her disposal not readily available to the Blackman. If she
is single she can some times easily move in with a girlfriend;
move back home or with another relative. If she has children
and falls on hard times she can get welfare, food stamps,
medical care and a place to live. She can get these benefits sick or well. No matter what her age. And she can get them literally for as long as she likes. The Blackman is in no such position. If he falls upon hard times it is difficult, if not impossible, for him to find a place where he is welcome to live with no money. Males do not readily take each other in
132
as women do. Blackwomen also do not take in a Blackman
who cannot pull his own weight financially. If he has had relationships with several women who he remains in contact
with, sometimes he can live with them for a short while
before they becomes impatient with his displacement and
starts making snide remarks about his being out of work.
His other option is to try to move back home, if he has
relatives, or to become homeless and live in a shelter or on
the streets as many Blackmen do. He certainly cannot move
back home as readily as a Blackwoman. His mother, a
Blackwoman, eventually feels the same way as other
Blackwomen, he should get out and get a job, any job, as
long as he makes some money, and he should get out and
get him a woman, or he should get out period. Blackwomen
give no consideration to the jobless rate of Blackmen and
boys in general. She is not the least bit concerned with, what
she considers false pride, about what kind of job he will
take, how much money he desires to earn, or how he must
be treated on a job in order to stay on it. He is expected to
suffer any kind of insults or vile treatment as long as he
makes some money. Any complaints he makes about why he
quit a job or refused to take one are considered to be lame
excuses for laziness. She has never been a Blackman in a
white society. She has no concept of what it is like. If she
lets him move in with her, or continue to live with her when
he loses his job, or quits his job, it’s not long before she
makes him feel like he is the lowest creature in a swamp, as
if the sum total of his self-worth is based on the dollar. This
is another contradiction because she claims she wants the
Blackman home with her, but only when she’s there and
only if he gives up some money. She takes the lessons of the
whore - if a man sleeps with her he owes her money for the
privilege. No romance without finance, or you have to pay
to play, are her slogans. If the man she is interested in does
not have the education that equals hers he must be at least a
self-made man and have a business or inherited money, or
drug money, or other - but he must have some money to
make up for his scholastic ignorance - or - the potential to be
taught a little class according to her idea. She expects the
uneducated Blackman to be extra special in bed. He must be
able to do something that she can use as an explanation to
her friends about why she is with him. She does not think of
133
telling them that she loves him for being himself, or for his
values, or because he loves her and treats her well.
When not working at all everything he says is examined
with suspicion because she thinks if he isn’t working his
opinion and authority is invalidated and void until he gets
another job. No job. No respect. She may even lose interest
in him sexually because she claims she can’t get her mind off
bills. When he is not working or paying her in some way
she thinks that she is providing sex for free.
Broadening the spectrum, if the Blackman quits or gets
fired from his “good” job the Blackwoman panics more than
he does. She calls her parents and everyone else she knows
to inform them of the bad news. All of these people advise
her about how to handle it as if she were the wounded party.
She immediately becomes worried that she and/or the
children will starve to death. She launches a panic rocket
landing on the thought of her living in a barrel or some
where in the subway, instead of trying to comfort her man
and assure him that they can work something out. She may
mention the fact that she can get a part-time job (in addition
to her full-time one). Her financial security is easily shaken
because she can’t bear the thought that she might have to
change her style of living or give up a few of her material
things, and God forbid the neighbors from finding out. She
becomes convinced that if he got fired he must have done
something wrong, and whatever happened he should have
preserved his job. Most of them believe that their security is
working in the system for large white owned entities. Many
Blackmen never stop looking for the opening by which they
can escape a job owned by someone else and own one of his
own. His plight at that time is another whole tub of worms
and worry. His woman may or may not help him in his
quest to go for self. And if he should fail, and some ideas do
fail, she lets him know that he should have known that af
wouldn’t work in the first place. If she really does not like
his new business venture idea she will withhold her help.
The problems the Blackwoman presents the Blackman
with regarding her behavior in the workforce, or how she acts when she works on the same job as he does, or how she demonstrates her fierce competitive aura makes for bad vibes between them. He is sick and tired of all that women’s lib boldness and fed up with her aggressive actions.
134
The Blackman has responded to the Blackwoman’s aggression in the workforce by retaliating in the following ways:
1) He stays away from her
2) Hekeeps his contact superficial and platonic
3) He does not voluntarily help her financially
4) He has turned to the white woman and the
oriental woman for love
5) He declines and ducks marriage
6)e°He does not make a full emotional
commitment
7) He does not share his ideas with her
8) He becomes bitter towards all women in
general
9) And in many cases he withdraws from her bed
10) He does not plan on a future that includes her
The Blackwoman has the misplaced notion that she can
treat the Blackman any way that she pleases and that he has
to take it if he wants the sex. This has proven, once again, to
be another failed plan because the Blackman while mostly
silent, is using the non-violent skills listed above to get back
at her. She in turn becomes more adamant in her charges
against him and the stand off continues. The Black woman is
convinced that she must go for self and achieve her own
personal goals: She will fully compete with the Blackman to
prove her superiority and flaunts among the society
bellowing her achievements. Professional gain has taken
precedence over her male relationship. The Blackman does
not have any special worth to her. He is just another
opponent who persists in getting in her way as she mounts
the glistening rungs of the corporate ladder. Any
advancement outside of the home is considered high
achievement, no matter how many casualties she causes.
When she is criticized for competing with the Blackman for
the best jobs under the whiteman’s employment system she
dismisses her evil intent and claims that the Blackman is just
a sore loser and not as qualified as she is. She gives less
than a damn about his need to have adequate funds to
support his family. She wants accolades from her coworkers and other strangers and she wants material things
that cost cash money. She believes she can outdo the
135
Blackman in every category and she takes pride in being
labeled as a tough bitch on the job. And she takes special
pleasure in her never ending plight to prove her equality with
the Blackman, the white woman and the white man. She
feels inadequate to all three groups and is often compelled to
achieve higher pay and higher recognition as a defense
mechanism. All of this is not to say that the Blackwoman
should not work outside the home, but a job does promote
the idea of independence and separation - especially in the
brass laden carpeted corridors of the corporate world. If she
does not know what to expect, and plan how she will defer
negative stumbling blocks, she is doomed to become a
victim of peer pressure and change for the worse. Any
change she makes contrary to what her man wants and
expects of her will be detrimental,
If working outside the home interferes with child
rearing, taking care of her husband and attending the house,
the Blackwoman should only work part-time or quit the job
altogether. Yes, it is a sacrifice. But it is a major way to put
the Blackman back in the spot of head of the house and
breadwinner. The Blackman should access the situation,
decide how he wants to live and whether or not her financial
contribution is worth he and the children giving up what they
need from the wife and mother in his home. If he approves
of her working then he should cooperate with her in
maintaining the home and taking care of the children. It
doesn’t have to be 50-50, but he should help.
OVERTIME
On the issue of the Blackwoman working overtime,
please know that Blackwomen want to please their bosses.
Many of them will work late into the evening and into the
night, Saturdays or Sundays, or full weekends. Obviously
to do this she must neglect other areas of her life that requixé
care and maintenance. Cooking dinner and cleaning the house - spending time with her man often go lacking due to her hectic overtime schedule. No Blackwoman should become dedicated to her job or so-called career if it requires her to neglect her family. As priority stressed, mealtime is a very important event in the Black household. All schedules should be adjusted whenever possible to allow for mealtime congregating of the family members. This is similar to an
136
every evening reunion of the group. If the Blackwoman
finds it unavoidable to get out of working overtime she
should prepare all her meals on the weekend and freeze them
so they can be reheated each weekend evening for dinner, or
get up earlier each morning to fix the dinner meal. Under no
circumstances should the Blackman and his children have to
eat junk food or scuffle around the kitchen on their own just
so the Blackwoman can work overtime on someone else’s
job. Her first job and her first responsibility is to her man
and family. Small children especially need their mother home
with them in the evening and weekends should somehow be
devoted to some relaxed family time or outside activities
together. Of course it is impossible to maintain this schedule
every weekend, but at least once a month the family should
participate in a group activity for communal enjoyment. And
yes, she will rebel against the Blackman informing her that
she needs to cut her overtime down. It is also wise at this
time that he ask her what she intends to do with the extra

Chapter 14
ATTITUDES
Blackwomen very easily do what is referred to as “get an
attitude” or “catching an attitude,” and this means routinely,
daily, moment to moment, she may decide for whatever
reason that it is necessary to float between good feelings and
bad feelings which equals an: attitude. The attitude
represents a change in mood. She may become dark and
gloomy, or uptight for days on end, about something that
she has not even presented to the Blackman. There are
several interactions she predetermines will give her some
kind of emotional problem that results in her exhibiting and
attitude. The attitude is unpleasant, makes her extra hard to
get along with and pervades the atmosphere with
dissatisfaction. The Blackwoman’s attitude affects how she
looks, how she responds and what she does. Her attitude
can be described simply as her state of mind - negative.
While her presence is important - and her cooperation
essential - the most important issue the Blackman has to deal
137
with is the Blackwoman’s state of mind. If she has an
attitude she will be careless, cynical, silent and disagreeable
to live with in peace. The first hint that she has an attitude is
that she becomes quiet, distracted and irritable. The
Blackwoman has a very short memory of the good times she
has with a Blackman, and as soon as something goes wrong
she immediately figures out what it would take for her to bail
out of the relationship. She can make her exit plans in the twinkling of an eye
and instantly is ready to do whatever it takes to flee. She
keeps these plans ready in the forefront of her mind anyway
because of her insecurity, distrust and lack of faith in the
Blackman. Once she’s figured out her game plan, i.e., what
she’ll do if he does this or that, she becomes settled. It is at
that moment her confidence returns and she is ready to deal
with the problem at hand. She deals with personal interaction
problems as if they are happening during the last day of her
life. There is no tomorrow for she and the man she is at odds
with.
The unfair thing about the Blackwoman’s attitude is that
she chooses to act out her dissatisfaction instead of
confronting the Blackman head-on honestly and explaining
what she is unhappy about. Sometimes once the actual
words are spoken out of her mouth she realizes how
ridiculous her position is, and hopes with all her might the
man will offer a simple solution or explanation that will get
her off the hook. When she is carrying an attitude about
something she thinks the entire conversation out in her head.
She may even rehearse her lines or make up responses to
what she thinks the Blackman will say. She may become so
involved with her inner-talk scripts she is unable to hear
other people talk to her and appears to be daydreaming. The
Blackman, who is unaware of her self-talks and verbal
preparation, may be surprised at the almost instant intensity
and organization she presents her problem in. While she is
waiting to see if the man will figure out for himself what is

knows about him and possibly his whole family. Her
friends, other Blackwomen, are, of course, sympathetic with her predicament. The Blackman is literally tried, and convicted before he even goes on trial. Later on after the
138
conflict has passed and she and the man are back on good

exaggerate. Her friends may tell her to continue to watch him

she can never really be completely believed about how happy
she and her man are. The reputation of a Blackman hangs in
a delicate balance between his woman and her girlfriends.
Once ruined by rumor he has a hard time regaining their
confidence or respect. He never knows this because usually
they all continue to act friendly never letting on that they
have changed their opinion about him because of something
he allegedly did to his woman. They are compassionate with
each other about having an attitude.
If the Blackman examines the Blackwoman when she
has an attitude, one she denies she is having, he can see the
physical symptoms of her stress such as:
1) General irritability and gloom, she sulks,
may manage a strained smile and gets
impatient easily.
2) Her heart pounds - she shows symptoms of
being excited or out of breath, her blood
pressure may rise.
3) Her mouth and throat becomes dry - she may
have difficulty speaking, or responding in a
normal tone.
4) She may throw things around, she becomes
careless, developing an “I don‘t care” or “Fit” manner.
5) Dizziness - she may appear slightly drunk
when she walks.
6) She develops an inability to concentrate on
normal activities, she may have to be spoken
to several times before she responds.
139
7) Floating anxiety - she appears to be afraid
and nervous while restraining her outburst.
8) Trembling or nervous ticks - her jaw or eye
may twitch, her hands may shake from trying
to control her outrage.
9) Stuttering - she may jumble her words or
display other speech difficulties.
10) Grinding her teeth - she may do this while
awake or asleep at night. She might start to

11) Diarrhea, indigestion or vomiting - caused by

12) Popping pills or increased smoking - she may

13) Severe headaches - sick migraines that may

14) Drinking - increased alcohol consumption,
she may drink more and say things she later

15) Nightmares - she may wake up with cold
sweats or toss and turn.
16) Accident prone - because of her distraction
she may start bumping into things or drive
carelessly and becomes dizzy. She cannot see
because her vision is clouded with her
problems.
The Blackwoman does not know that stress is a reaction
caused by the value she places on something. If she changes
her value or reaction to an issue or subject she will have
milder responses. If she tells herself that certain things make
her upset undoubtable they will.
Sometimes the Blackman will stay away from the
Blackwoman until she gets over her attitude. Left to her own

head, settle on a decision she can live with and continue on

however, requires her to make a decision about whether or

140
problems as temporary stumbling blocks. Every issue is a
a or death problem that the entire relationship dangles rom.
During the course of her attitude she may also devise all

Blackwoman, if he stays with her long enough he will

quakes inside from fear wondering if his new interest means
he is bored with her and no longer wants her. She vies to be
the sum total of his focus and will send him through a lot of
fabricated changes just to keep his undivided attention.
The Blackwoman is not familiar with non-possessive
loving so she believes that to hold on to the love of the
Blackman she must cling to an invisible umbilical cord
attached to his brain. Because she is like the earth she desires
to be steady with her whole being circulating around the
Blackman. The Blackman is worldly, he may choose to visit
any of the other planets while still keeping his homestead

bases in numerous areas encompassing his universe. His
limitations are not equally set with the Blackwoman’s so
while she becomes content with her home routine, he may
charge on searching and discovering - everything.
The Blackwoman thinks that his experimentations and
investigations outside of her means that he does not love her
or want her. She wants the Blackman to be like she is,
satisfied and accepting of their life together. If he does not
do this she thinks he is wrong and will get mad at him. She
prefers to have him hanging on to her dress tail, but when he
141
does this she disrespects him, considers him weak and treats

Blackwoman but he must set limitations about what she can
and cannot do. She wants him to enforce his rule over her
even though she claims she doesn’t. The main process that creates security for the
Blackwoman is for the Blackman to tell her what to do, have
a plan for her life and see to it that she does it. She desires to
be convinced of his love and be forced to do right by him.
She is similar to a child who does something to see if the
parent will allow it, or does something wrong to see if the

time to reaffirm for themselves that the parent loves them
enough to reprimand them. The Blackwoman practices a
contradiction when she claims she wants to have her own
way. All the while she wants to be monitored and restricted
so she will not harm herself or others.
It’s deep.
She gets an attitude when she can’t have her way about
one of the Blackman’s ideas, she will be in disagreement

with. She should be reminded that it is better to go along

to win - and be right. If she chooses him she should go

support his ideas and go along with him then she should get
herself another man whom she will go along with. The only
right between the Blackman and Blackwoman is for them to
be together in unity, peace and love. Obviously, for the
Blackwoman these goals are far down the list of what she
says will make her happy. She thinks she knows about
everything in a more sane fashion than he. Her fight against
him is based solely on fear. Fear of abandonment, fear of
losing her home, fear of looking like a fool to her friends,
and fear of being used. She has been programmed to think
the worse of him by books, news reports, movies, television and newspapers - all her life. She is still not qualified to rule herself, let alone the Blackman, but she tries to force him by one method or another to accept her ideas about everything from work to lifestyle. She continues to fail. And she constantly has an attitude about anything related to her failure. Her daily state of being teed off about numerous
142
things increase the frown lines in her face and make her look haggard.
Sometime rather than submit to the Blackman and be
what he wants her to be some Blackwomen choose to be
alone. They justify their right to live in celibacy and
nowadays claim it as a welcome hiatus or satisfaction to be
looked forward to between one of her failed tries.
She claims being alone gives her time to get to know
herself and get in touch with her real feelings. It is
impossible for her to get to know herself without guidance.
When she is alone it only gives her time to congratulate
herself on being right about how she is. She wants to keep
her fragile ego in check so she only admits to light criticism

advisors in women magazines and newspaper columns have

Blackman. The options offered to lone women are
distractions presented to help the Blackwomen conclude she
doesn’t really need a man to be happy. Certainly a woman
will not keel over and die if she doesn’t have a man, but then
again, the moment of death is over in a second, while
loneliness seems to linger for infinity.
Any Blackwoman who wants to have a Blackman can
have one. Obviously it requires her to adjust her ideas and
stipulations. She must adjust her behavior to attract a man
and keep him. Being fine, having a grand education and
money are not sufficient credentials to attract, keep and
satisfy a Blackman. There are countless multitudes of
Blackwomen who possess these accoutrements who are
lonely and untouched by a man. They are the ones so rigid in
their requirements and refuse to budge of accept any offer
other than what she dreams a man should be. The man of her
dreams does not exist anywhere on earth except in her
cartoon-like imagination. There is no real shortage of
Blackmen if the Blackwoman would decide to deal with
what she has as opposed to living alone and dreaming of
what is not available to her.
Still, Blackwomen persist in pursuing a tragic wasted life
of trying to re-make the Blackman. She wants a gender-free
society where the women are free to act like men and the
men are free to take care of their own needs domestically like
a woman. She tantalizes him with her act of smiles and
sensitivity and then drops the bomb on him as soon as
anything happens that doesn’t please her. The bomb is her
funky disposition that interferes with her happiness.
Not only does the Blackwoman believe that trying to
please the Blackman is a blatant form of masochism, she has
sufficiently fooled many Blackmen into believing that there
is no longer any such thing as a “good woman.” She has
also inspired him to believe that he may as well stop
searching for what does not exist and to accept her lowdown decorum as the norm. She wants him to give up on
being happy the way he perceives it and accept the terms she
offers and be satisfied. Her greatest threat is that one day the
Blackman will learn the truth of her and bust her in her
deception. She becomes weary carrying the burden of
keeping the Blackman deaf and dumb. Day after day
juggling the details of falsehood and truth mixed together
and trying to maintain a natural looking facial expression. It
is time consuming and tiring but she thinks it is the only way
to relate to the Blackman in order to get any benefit for
herself. She does not view her actions as trickery but as a
necessary game plan to survive. She too, has given up on
the possibility of finding a good Blackman - not the kind she
is looking high and low for. She has three main tools she
uses to control him: 1) denial 2) aggression and 3)
jealousy. She can be counted on to use these three traits to
combat the Blackman when he does something that she feels
she absolutely cannot accept. It could be anything. She does
not apply the same tactics to every issue that arises. She
changes her position craftily according to whatever she feels
emotionally at the time of the infraction. Sometimes just ore
misplaced word in one of her man’s explanations can send her off. She is a real grouch when she is suffering from one of her attitudes.
It is a good idea for the Blackwoman to be taught to smile. Smile. Smile when she greets her man in the morning and smile when she meets him at night. A smile is a very helpful and comforting form of the communication of
144
confidence and satisfaction. She must be reminded to keep
up the happy spirit in the home and make it a happy place.
REGARDING HAPPINESS
It has been said by many Blackmen that it is impossible
to make a Blackwoman happy. They say “that woman ain’t
never satisfied” or “no matter what I do it still ain’t enough.”
It is reported that Blackmen make this complaint because
they have tried to do many things to make the Blackwoman
happy, satisfied and content. Unfortunately they seemingly
are destined to fail miserably each time they give it a try. For
some odd reason whenever the Blackwoman gets what she
wants and is happy, at least for the moment, she starts to
think that something must be wrong. She thinks that if it
looks like things are working out then something must be
going on that she doesn’t know about. At this stage she
starts to examine her life, check stuff out more closely and
nit-pick at her surroundings and the people in it. She will
watch and check until she comes up with something that she
can express her negative attitude about. She just can’t seem
to stand too much happiness, it makes her uncomfortable.
When the Blackman sees her in this kind of depression
he exclaims: “what is it now?” because he is accustomed to
her interrupting her own happiness with a new problem she
just thought of. The Blackman should realize that being
unhappy is a bad habit to her. She finds it extremely tedious
to break out of her old habits that make her life miserable.
She has tried many ways to be happy and invariably ends up
just as unhappy as she started out. The problem is that she
does not know what happiness is or how to express it freely.
Happiness is a state of mind that she is afraid to linger in too
long for fear that she will be overcome by something or
somebody. Since she is not used to being happy she does
not know how to act when she is.
Chapter 15

The lesbian Blackwoman is repelled by the secret normal

Blackwomen with normal sex drives who desire and

that none of them want to readily admit to. It’s too base to discuss. Too uncool. Some things can be omitted.
When in pursuit of an actual one-on-one sexual
encounter with a man the Blackwoman is very driven. She

Capacity to become a raging maniac. Her sexual program is

Chapter 16
THE OTHER WOMAN
The Blackman’s handling of more than one woman is in
a baaaaaaaaad state of condition. The tenderest part of a
Blackwoman’s life is the part that involves her relationship
with her man. Even though she is not doing what he wants
her to do, the fact that she is with him and claims to love him
makes her territorial. She may go literally berserk if her man wanders for a one night stand, or develops a full blown relationship with another woman. The Blackwoman does not understand her role in life with the Blackman. She can be the woman, or the other woman, to a Blackman. Both positions
154
have their own idiosyncrasies of pro and con. It depends upon the Blackwoman’s perspective as to which position she
will occupy. She does not know that whichever position she
takes does not eliminate the other position from the
Blackman’s life. She cannot bear even the thought of this.
While she does not obey the rules governing her duties
as a wife, she expects her man to obey all of the rules of the
marriage vows or of the monogamous commitment. She
refuses to accept the truth about what apparently is his
nature. There is no history of the Blackman settling down
with one woman and never desiring to have another one.
Before slavery, during slavery or after slavery. The
Blackman has never been recorded to have just one woman.
The Blackwoman does not know that just because her man
does not have another woman, does not mean that he does
not want one, he just has not figured out how to do it and
keep the peace. He knows for an actual fact that if his
woman finds out, or even suspects, that he is interested in
adding another woman to his life she will not give him
another moment of peace until he has convinced her beyond
all doubt that he has either changed his mind or wasn’t
serious about it in the first place. She will keep him up all
night if need be until he gets it straight. Since the
Blackwoman has reduced her own worth to her genitals, she
also equates the Blackman’s value with his. She has an idea
that sexual intercourse has some kind of divine worth to the
Blackman as it has to her, and that he should only reserve it
for one woman - herself. The Blackman seemingly is under
no such interpretation and is amazed that she reacts so
violently to him having another woman. He knows she is
jealous. She becomes furious beyond belief and may have to
be restrained physically from harming him or herself. Her
first charge is that “you lied to me” which is followed by
“why didn’t you tell me?” as if his telling her would make
any difference in her reaction. Number one, he knows he
has to lie because he knows she can’t handle much truth
about the reality of his life, much less the fact that he desires
another woman but still wants her. She calls it two-timing.
She will demand all sorts of explanations, none of which
appease her distraught emotional state. Sometimes she
demands the Blackman to tell her every sordid detail of the
relationship, describe the woman, how he met her, whose
155
idea it was, where it happened, how many times it
happened, did he use protection, and she will compare dates
to see if he had her on the same day or night he had her.
Usually the man will give her a portion of the answers to the

would never recover. She wants him to explain it in such a
way that proves that he was seduced by this horrible other
tramp of a woman, or that it only happened one time, or that
she belongs to another man anyway and it will never happen
again. She wants to believe that the sexual encounter was
brief, uninvolved, superficial and consisted of only straight
sex that was over in a few minutes. The intensity of her
reaction is based on the magnitude of the value she places on
sex. If she and her man are having extremely good sex she

world, no matter how long the relationship has existed,
based on what her man does with his sex organ. A quagmire

of being with other women, several children and the

Part of the Blackwoman’s frustration is that she is

They are not equipped to handle two husbands, two sets of

It is a mystery to her how the Blackman manages to do

On examination these are some of the most popular
sayings of today. They seem harmless enough. They have
been passed down from one generation to the next. They are
sets of words that need no explanation when applied to a
relationship between a man and a woman. They have
endured and provided people with catch-all responses and
