any other memorized text.
She should be told the truth about everything, not the
shuffling half-truth, but any truth that affects her life and will
give the Blackman peace and more freedom in his life. One
thing is for sure, the Blackman does not have to worry that
he is the only one who has been lying, the Blackwoman is
just as guilty of lying as he is. Their subjects are different,
but the effects of the lies are the same: disunity, suspicion
and doubt.
Another area of confusion she participates in is the
difference between what an affair is and what a relationship
is. An affair is exactly what it suggests, a temporary
happening, something attended, a thing to do, an event. In
more precise terms, an affair is strictly an episode or many
episodes of sex without responsibility. Fornication.
Adultery, on the other hand, is sex expressed when one
is not sure of one’s womanhood or manhood. Adultery is
sex used as a proving ground to try to convince self of
capability and usefulness. A relationship is a union of a man
and woman where they are responsible for each other, not
necessarily financially, but emotionally, mentally and
physically. A relationship is grounded in reality. Life
situations are met with discussion and compassion, and:-there
164
is demonstrated caring, involvement and consideration.
Certainly a real relationship presents more challenges than a
fun-filled, frolic in the hay limited affair. Affairs are easier,
less complicated and represent immaturity, cowardices and
lust - raw passion with no real potential for love. It is a
limited engagement that dies when the passion burns out or
becomes impossible to satisfy. A relationship is unlimited,
fruitful, educational and filled with daily excitement. It is
unselfishness in its most desirable form between a man and a
woman.
The Blackwoman and the Blackman must get past the
stage of becoming addicted to sex, the possibility of sex and
the temporary euphoria of genital to genital contact. Yes, sex
is big fun, but it’s bigger fun when there is commitment and
a full knowledge of the other person far greater than face
value and physical compatibility. To be selective of a mate is
not being a prude, it is being mature, intelligent and
considerate. The Blackwoman must choose her man, accept
her lot and work to make him happy and be satisfied with
her choice. There are no perfect choices among
Blackwomen, and there are no perfect choices among
Blackmen. But all of the choices are potentially acceptable,
workable and capable of allowing both parties to have a
measure of happiness and security. Things are only right to a
certain extent and the Blackwoman must work to achieve the
fullest extent of happiness and satisfaction available to her. It
is whatever she makes of it.
Chapter 17
ARGUMENTS
The Blackman knows that arguing with a Blackwoman is
a useless exercise and one that he cannot win. Nobody on
earth can out argue a Blackwoman. She may prepare for an
argument days, weeks or years in advance rehearsing her
strategies of how to get her points across. She will save up
certain incriminating information to use it at the proper
moment to give her leverage. She can speak very quickly
and fire off insults hundreds at a time like a gattling gun. The
Blackwoman, when arguing with the Blackman, tends to
165
sound as if she is fussing at her child. She does not
distinguish between the two when she is angry. Especially
since she views the Blackman as an incompetent immature
being. Many a Blackman has become outdone and tonguetied from trying to argue back at a Blackwoman. She is a
master at it. She will harbor grudges and hold fast to a
hostile thought forever. She is very adept at negative
thinking therefore when it comes time to argue she uses the
no-holds-barred theory and vents all of her complaints at
once. She will throw in new topics, things that she has been
upset about over the past month, and is glad to have the
chance to get it off. She will make the most cruel and throatcutting remarks. And in every new argument she throws up
the old embers from the previous one. She will pout until
she gets her way. A mere apology from her man is not
enough. Nothing is enough if she is mad and thinks she’s
right. She might demand some material item like clothes,
jewelry, money or more until she is satisfied that her man is
truly sorry and repentant. Sometimes it might be a request
that he do certain housework, take her out, keep the children
or stay at home. This can only happen when the Blackman
admits to some wrongdoing or lies and she knows it a lie but
she lets him get away with it. Sometimes the admission is
not verbal. It may be a look. When arguing she insists on
having the last word. She curses and flings the most vile
words out of her mouth. Sometimes she can keep up the
same argument for days never changing a word. She
manages to stay angry for an extraordinary length of time,
will huff and puff, turn red, sweat, stomp her feet and slam
doors or throw things. After a certain point she ceases to
address the right or wrong of the issue and merely fights to
win. She considers herself the winner if she manages to
drive the man out of the house, make him stop talking back
or get him to concede. If she has done something andws
guilty, busted, she checks her mental files to find something
that the Blackman has done which is equally wrong and use
that point to defend herself. She cannot rest until she forces him to admit his error.
She has learned that the Blackman is peaceful by nature.
She has learned that he will go along with a lot of situations
just to keep the peace and keep from arguing with. her. Sometimes she places outrageous demands on him to try to
166
perk him into an argument. When she finds he will
acquiesce to desensitize the situation, she will press further
just to see how far she can push him before he rebels. It’s a
game to her, and an unfair one, since she could not survive
this kind of mental pressure from the Blackman. But she is
petty and she keeps all sorts of little mental score cards, and
when the right moment presents itself she lets go with both
barrels. Below are a few examples of the trite reasons the
Blackwoman uses to get mad at and argue with the
Blackman:
1) If he stays away from home too long (whether
she knows where he is or not).
2) If he visits with his mother or other family
members too often or too long.
3) If he does not take her to the place of her choice.
4) If he breaks a date with her for a reason she does
not accept.
5) If he says “I'll be right back” and doesn’t.
6) For doing more for his side of the family than
hers.
7) For speaking to an old acquaintance with too
much enthusiasm.
8) For dancing too close with another woman.
9) For looking at another woman when she is out
with him
10) For not telling her that he loves her enough
11) For spending too much money on his “other”
children.
12) For not complimenting her on her new outfit or
hairstyle.
13) For not remembering her birthday or other
special occasions
14) For being too tired or busy to have sex with her.
15) For admiring another Blackwoman on T.V. or in
the movies.
16) For not being on time for a dinner or date.
17) For working too much, not cutting the grass, or
emptying the trash.
18) For not attending a special affair with her.
19) For not going to church or on a church bus trip.
20) For being too busy to listen to her chatter,
preoccupation.
167
For not opening the door or pulling out her chair.
For wearing a suit she doesn’t like, or any other
outfit.
For doing too many favors for his friends.
For loaning money to his friends or relatives.
For watching too much television or VCR.
For not continuing his education.
For not asking for a raise.
For buying a car she doesn’t like.
For wanting to have some time with the boys or
the fellas.
For ignoring her accidentally when he is with
another woman.
For not showing his affection for her in public.
For growing a beard, getting a special haircut.
For taking the children’s side against her.
For showing out or dancing funny at a party.
For telling her to shut up or that she talks too
much.
For telling her that her clothes look funny or too
tight.
For telling her she has on too much make-up.
For asking her to clean up the house.
For expecting her to give him some attention.
For not going shopping with her.
For going to sleep in a movie or while she is
talking to him.
For not letting her drive or give him traffic
instructions.
For staying out too late, not reporting in or
answering questions.
For telling her that she nags too much.
For forbidding her to do a certain thing or goa
certain place. .
For asking her where she’s been. iy Da
For asking her to fix him something to eat.
For refusing to baby-sit the children when he has
other plans.
For asking her what she did with her (or his) money.
For telling and proving to her she is wrong about something.
These 50 categories are just a few of the often trite
examples that the Blackwoman blames as reasons to get mad
at the Blackman. This list is actually almost infinite and just
as ridiculous. This is not to say that couples do not become
displeased with each other for doing this - or - that, but the
displeasure must be expressed in civilized terms. A civilized
person is one who does and says things in an intelligent,
mannerable, considerate way. Civilized more importantly
means: One possessing knowledge, wisdom and
understanding, culture, refinement and is not savage. The
pursuit of happiness.
There is another kind of Blackwoman who does not
consider a fight a fight unless some licks are passed. She is
the kind who believes that she is just as tough and bad as a
man, and thinks she can say whatever she wants to, no
matter how repugnant, insulting and disrespectful it is - and
thinks there are no ramifications or accountability. She tries
to be a bully. She braces up to her man toe to toe and eye to
eye. She presents a problem when she does this. This brings
up a very unpopular and unpleasant category - the
Blackman’s self-defense mechanisms. There is never an
excuse for ever hitting a Blackwoman anywhere but in the
mouth. Because it is from that hole, in the lower part of her
face, that all her rebellion culminates into words. Her
unbridled tongue is a main reason she cannot get along with
the Blackman. She often needs a reminder. This does not
mean that she needs, or wants, to be battered or beaten to a
bloody pulp. However, if she ignores the authority and
superiority of the Blackman, there is a penalty. When she
crosses this line and becomes viciously insulting it is time
for the Blackman to soundly slap her in the mouth.
She is often like a rambunctious child. She wants to
know what her parameters are. If she is allowed to curse at
and yell at the Blackman she will get worse. This is not an
instruction to be used as a license to daily beat the hell out of
the Blackwoman. Frustration and mental illness has already
been identified as reasons the Blackwoman is physically
battered and abused by the Blackman. This directive is for
sensible Blackmen who adopt the tactics and advice of this
entire text. The author is not advising: “when in doubt,
knock her out.” To beat the Blackwoman and injure her, or
169
to strike her with a closed masculine fist is forbidden. For
the Blackman to become impatient and just beat the
Blackwoman is savage and uncivilized behavior that‘is not
recommended. If she is brutalized repeatedly the relationship will fail
and the Blackman will possibly be arrested and charged with
cowardly abusing a woman. This is not the goal. Make no
mistake about it. No black-eyes, no punching in the breast or
stomach, no stomping and no upper-cuts . Analyzing the
options available to the Blackman when the Blackwoman’s
mouth becomes uncontrollably disrespectful, they total only
a few:
1) He can walk away from her, leave the house, get
drunk.
2) Hecan go to his other woman and complain.
3) Hecan quit the relationship, leave the woman
4) Hecan try to argue back - and lose.
5) Hecan seek counseling or sympathy from friends.
6) Or he can offer her a sound open-handed slap in
the mouth.
She may also have to be physically restrained until her
anger and shock passes. It’s okay to restrain her, she won’t
burst and no Blackwoman can win a physical fight against a
right Blackman. The Blackman should not be so refined or
cool to accept harsh language from his woman. Soon she
will become trained and curb her vicious tongue when
talking to him. She will gain a new respect for him and
finally realize when she is exceeding the limits.
She can cause a great commotion when she calls herself
“getting mad,” and has been known to turn the house topsyturvy, report the Blackman’s illegal practices to the police,
tell his parents some deep dark secret about him, call his
boss and defame his occupational reputation, slash his tirés,
burn his clothes, or do anything else she thinks will first
embarrass him, then inconvenience him, and then make him
madder than he has ever been in his life. She becomes
enveloped in her dissension for one main purpose: to pay him back! She will even destroy items she herself has
bought for him. She’ll cry and scream and scratch - like a
wild animal - and she must be dealt with as such.
170
After she has done all she can humanly do she sits back
and waits on the Blackman’s reaction, she still wants his
attention, any kind of attention she can get, even if it is the
kind of attention that gets her butt whipped. She wants a
rumpus, a blow out, an emotional release. If it is possible to
restrain her long enough to get her to listen, it is possible that
she can be reasoned with. At this time the Blackman should
not lie. Repeat: the Blackman should not lie, he should tell
the truth, untampered with, and in such a way as to make her
understand that it is not the end of the world just because she
doesn’t like something that happened. Whenever she fusses
or battles with her man and wins she is still unhappy inside.
It is a very confusing dilemma she perpetrates because she
doesn’t really want to win, and whenever she loses she
gains more confidence in her man’s abilities. She is so
mixed up. Whenever she wins it muddles her more. It is not
that important to her - whatever he does, as long as he can
give her the following:
1) Companionship - she wants to be sure that he
will not abandon her. She wants belongingness,
to be possessed by him.
2) She wants to be connected, included and
considered.
3) Opportunity to give service - She wants the
Blackman to give her something to do that will
help him, she wants to feel part of him by doing
things for him especially designed for her that no
one else can do as well. She wants to be expected
to do certain things.
4) She wants a home - She wants a place to live that
is secure for her children. She wants her home to
be her sanctuary.
5) She wants sexual fulfillment - She wants the
Blackman to desire her and enjoy her sexual
expressions and tell her so. She wants to be
touched and stroked.
6) She wants to be protected - She wants to be
confident that her life and her children’s lives are
safe with the Blackman, she wants him to
demonstrate this publicly if possible.
7) She wants her man to make decisions - She
wants him to decide what she needs and give it to
171
her, she does not want to have to figure out
everything for herself. She wants him to TAKE
charge.
8) | She wants him to be firm - She wants hin to set
the limits and enforce his instructions and rules.
Not by breaking the peace.
9) She wants security - Again, she wants to know
that her man will not leave her for another
woman as long as she performs properly, and
that he will always come back to her.
10) She wants him to be spiritual - She wants her
man to identify with and recognize a higher
authority (God) and guide his life by his religious
principles.
If the Blackman gives her all or some of the above
requests, after a time, she will become secure and give him
peace. It is extremely tenuous to try to give her the things
that make for peace because she has been taught to live in a
mental hell daily. There are those who are idea producers
and planners who give directions, and those who agree to
follow the instructions to make the goals a reality. This
system is the only one that works to make progress in
anything. The Blackman’s problem is that the Blackwoman
thinks she is the one who has the ideas and is qualified to
give the directions. Well, she has been able to lead other
Blackwomen and some other white people, but she has not
been able to lead the Blackman. He won’t go. He cannot by
nature follow the Blackwoman. It does not matter what she
has, who she is or what she gives him. He will not follow
the Blackwoman as his guide. He would rather mark his
place, stand still, or step backwards. But he will not go
forward with her in the front.
The Blackwoman has been tutored by the liberation
seeking white woman to execute her own individuality and
throw off the shackles of husbandly bondage, and practice
self-freedom. While some of this may be true of the white
woman’s plight, it has no bearing on the Blackwoman’s
situation since she has not been under the Blackman’s rule
since prior to slavery. Slavery was such a desperate time for
Blacks due to the race destructing tactics employed by the
slavemaster. Not only was there unbearable physical pain
172
and excruciating demanding labor; but separation was
promoted by offering singular and individual rewards gained
by pleasing the slavemaster and being a good slave. A good
slave was one who consented to do anything, and did
everything possible to stay alive. The individual-reward
system ripped apart the ties of couples, sibling, relatives and
friends. Independent self-preservation was the rule - if one
wanted to stay alive unhurt. This training was a fallacy
because the real truth is that the greatest advancements are
made through unity and agreement not individuality. She is
beautiful butterfly encased in a cocoon of fear, doubt and
suspicion. She has been frightened and allowed her fears to
govern her life and behavior. It will take a long time to get
her back on the nght path to successful Blackwomanhood
and it is a journey she cannot make without the guidance of
the Blackman.
Blackwomen seemingly have a bloodline connection
when it comes to taking and following instructions. She is
hardheaded and stubborn to have her own way. She thinks
that if she obeys her man that he will try to make a fool of
her and take away her identity. She also fears what her
family and friends will say if they find out she lets him tell
her what to do. Therefore she is disobedient.
Obedient means one who is willing to follow instruction
as a means of respect.
Slave means a human being who is owned by another
and dominated to the point of drudgery, mistreatment and
pain.
Fool means a silly person, capable of being tricked or
deceived.
Blackwomen are not readily able to explain why they
associate obedience with that of being a fool. Nor are they
able to elaborate on why they compare being told what to do
with slavery. Here again, is the psychological result of
slavery on the Blackwoman. The slavemaster systematically
and graphically devised steps to prove in front of the
Blackwoman that the Blackman had no control of his will.
And he destroyed anything he tried to build or protect. The
Blackman’s stamina, faith and perseverance in the face of
multiple, impossible looking obstacles, impressed her that he
was a soft-headed man who repeatedly deceives himself by
believing that he can do great things. It’s a paradox to her.
173
She doesn’t know that every civilization has rules.
Mathematically, to add means to multiply and increase; to
subtract means to divide and separate. One unites, the other
disbands. To unite means to come together. It is impossible
to have unity ahd separation at the same time. Yet, the
Blackwoman will practice separation while living in the same
house with the Blackman. She maintains her own separate
ideas, separate money, separate personal goals and separate
authority. She does not want anyone telling her what to do.
She thinks she is the most qualified to chart the course. If the
Blackman lets her rule him she does so with an iron fist.
Daily insults, threats, ignoring his presence, telling him what
to do every step of the way, abusive language, denial of sex
and respect. He has to be home after work at exactly the
same time everyday, he has to clean the house, take care of
his own clothes, wash dishes, fix his own food, maybe
sleep in another room, stay in every night, not spend any
money without her permission - and throughout all of this
she requires that he act like he likes it. She calls this keeping
him under control. His life becomes pure hell and totally
against his nature and she thinks this is the way to have a
successful relationship with a Blackman. These are her peace
requirements.
On the other side juxtaposing her position, she has some
inner feelings that represent disdain for herself. She hates the
Blackman for letting her treat him this way. She wants him
to stand up and defend himself even if it means he has to
knock her out of the way to do so. This is necessary
whenever the Blackwoman steps out of the protection of
womanly behavior and enters the dangerous domain of
masculine challenge. Regretfully, some Blackwomenwantto
be physically controlled. They may call each other up on the
phone and proudly brag to each other about how my man:
“beat my a--” or “beat the s--- out of me” or “kicked my a--,”
These confessions do not usually mean she was brutally
attacked or injured bodily, they just mean that they got
busted in something that other Blackwomen understand -
and realize the risk they take everyday keeping up the game.
A bust can be devastating too. But masochism is not new to her. Sometimes her sexual desires become perverted to the point that she derives a weird pleasure out of being hit because she likes the special attention she gets during make174
up. Some of them report that sex is extra good right after a good fight.
The Blackman should not give in to her abnormal
distortion of sexual stimulation connected to pain. Not
unless they both agree that this is how they like it. And both
parties go through these motions on a voluntary basis. The
Blackwoman does not know why certain kinds of pain and
discord appeal to her. She doesn’t know why she chooses
misery over happiness. She gives no consideration to the
roots of her ideas. She thinks that everything she thinks
comes directly from deep inside her nature, and that
whatever she feels is a permanent part of her. She learned
everything she knows from other people in the form of
instruction or suggestion. The majority of her instructors did
not have her well-being in mind.
She must be encouraged to talk and study her own
behavior. To trace her ideas to the place or point she took
them on. She must learn to recognize the signs of
backsliding. It takes a great deal of objectivity to study self,
but she can be taught to do so. Everyone must be taught to
do so if change is desired. The principles of how to think
and use the brain must be studied so the brain of the
Blackwoman can be reactivated with new ideas of peace and
love for the Blackman.
Arguments and disagreements between the Blackman
and the Blackwoman must be settled in such a way as to deal
with not just the catalyst issues that sparked the anger, but
why the particular incident invited such a frantic and hostile
reaction from the woman. Sometimes seemingly harmless or
lightweight events cause great eruption in the Blackwoman’s
mood and cause her to go out of control. The inside, true
issues and reasons must be delved into to get to the root of
her insecurity. Actually, to the Blackwoman, certain
behaviors in the Blackman are symbolic of things that caused
her unbearable pain and disappointment in her childhood or
teenage years. So when anything similar or reminiscent of
these occurrences appear in her personal relationship, which
she is already unsure of, she goes berserk. She feels
vulnerable, threatened, powerless and scared. These are
strong emotions when the Blackwoman experiences them
simultaneously.
In examining the Blackwoman’s childhood the Blackman
will have ample opportunity to scrutinize his own. All must
focus on the forces, the individual personalities and
environments from which they emerged. From these
memories they will be able to examine together the long term
effects of slavery and hard times. It will invariably be found
that the disparity between the Blackman and the
Blackwoman was already present when they were born. It
has existed a very long time, with each generation
contributing their own part to the decline of Black male and
Black female interpersonal relationships. It is not really
something that the present day Blacks invented. It is an
inherited habit of neglect, dissatisfaction and perversion. The
aforementioned three habits are not rules, nor are they
prerequisites to living in today’s world. They are patterns of
behavior brought on by suggestions and conclusions drawn
by Western societal morals and mores.
In the meantime, remember that the Blackwoman’s real
self is a wonderful loving helpmate, and if she forgets this
and behaves other than her real self, the Blackman should
collar her on the spot and insist she get it back together. He
must treat her right and make her treat him right. If after the
Blackman tries every way he can to make the Blackwoman
happy, does everything he feels necessary to make her
satisfied; and she still insists on being out of order and
rebellious, he should get rid of her. No matter what it costs.
He should not waste his precious life trying to live with a
Blackwoman who refuses to make him happy. Freedom and
peace are priceless.
There will arise a great resistance against the
recommendations in this chapter. Black and white women
will flail against the mere idea of suggesting that the
Blackman defend himself physically against the verbal
attacks of the Blackwoman. They will cite cases of
Blackwomen who have been battered by mentally unstable
Blackmen. But this directive is not for mentally unstable
Blackmen. It is for serious minded adult Blackmen who are in control of all their faculties and whose mission is to re- claim his place as the man over his woman, and who recognizes the absolute need for moderation and self-control. Until the argument issue is settled and until the Blackman is proclaimed the national winner, the Blackwoman’ will
176
continue to talk rough to her man. He must make her respect him. And she must live and behave in such a way as to make him respect her. This is called mutual respect. The Blackman must not rule his wife and family with an unrelenting iron
fist. He must be loving, understanding, flexibie (on minor
issues) and willing to confront and work on the visible and
invisible problems lurking in his household. When he
functions in this way he is in the best position to reprimand or punish wrongdoing.
Chapter 18
CONCLUSION
Every chapter in this book is filled with examples of the
kinds of problems the Blackwoman has both emotionally
and mentally. Until the Blackman is able to collectively
access the amount of psychological damage done to the
character of the Blackwoman he cannot know where or how
to begin correcting it. It’s deep. It’s an infection that for
many years has robbed the Blackman from reaching his full
potential as the king of kings. He is held back by his queen
who is deeply entrenched in fear. Fear that she will be
recognized and required to change. She is trying to run away
from the problem. She is trying to flee from the individual
work that must be done to save out nation. Although not lazy
by nature, she has become loose and careless, about herself,
and about her man and family. Her brain is smaller than the
Blackman’s so while she is acclaimed for her high scholastic
achievement, her thought processes do not compare to the
conscious Blackman’s. Her complaints and whining about
the lack of qualified Blackmen has led her to whitemen,
celibacy and lesbianism. She would rather live in the
artificial stages of these types of random, poorly thought out
choices than accept the options offered to her by the
Blackman. As the first, the original, and the leader, the
Blackman is responsible for remaking the Blackwoman into
what she must become.
It is not the easiest job, and payment will be slow and the
initial benefits few. The recipient challenges the work that
must be done. She is hard to train and it will take time,
177
patience, endurance and attention. Great sacrifices reap great
results.
Occasionally there surfaces a Blackwoman who strives
to be civilized. She is refined, intelligent, clean, courteous
and cheerful. It is a lucky Blackman who finds or makes this
kind of Blackwoman. She represents good material to-work
with . She requires guidance but because of her being in line
with the true nature of the Blackwoman she is easier to train
and submit herself for correction. This type of woman
accepts her responsibilities as wife and as mother. She is
satisfied and content with what her Blackman provides for
her and her family. Money is not her God, she lives a
simpler existence, teaches her children good values and
spends time with them to influence their futures. She is
obedient and respectful to her man, pursues wholesome
goals, is respected in her community and dresses in a stylish
non-seductive way. She does not curse or be loud in public,
she supports her man and enjoys the splendor of a happy sex
life. This kind of Blackwoman is a rarity. But she does
exist.
The Blackman’s work on the Blackwoman must be
carefully done so as not to permanently maim her ego. He
must approach and challenge her behavior head on, and he
must do it in such a way that will not drive her insane. He
must do it in a way, possibly different, in each situation
according to the degree of her problem. His words must not
be based on the rationale and reasoning of the American way
of life. Instead, his way must be one of his very own design
based on his individual needs and desires - whatever he
wants his woman, children and home to be like.
His standards for making these decisions must not be
calculated necessarily by the current American lifestyles. He
must figure out how to maintain a family and household in
peace. A household where there is love and sincere caring
for each of the members. He must control his group (his
family) by being civilized in his asking and telling. The
initial introduction of a new way of living will be a little rocky but this will subside in about three weeks. It takes approximately three weeks to make a change. If the Blackman can maintain his seriousness of purpose for just three weeks he will start to reap the benefits of change. .
178
The Blackwoman should be the proudest female on
earth, and she should feel she is the most blessed of all humankind because she is the mate of the greatest form of life on the planet - the Blackman. Instead of opting for this
prestigious position she has chosen the role of rebellion and
hostility. But she is not herself. Herself is the most valuable
and the most beautiful vessel the Blackman owns. She has
no match.
Also remember that much covered in this text concerning
the problems of the Blackwoman discusses variable levels of
indoctrination. Certainly not all Blackwomen suffer from all
of these traits to the fullest extent. But rest assured that all of
them are tainted on some level with every ailment mentioned.
Covertly, overtly, or both.
These are very serious times. Black lives are at risk from
the deadly extreme of drugs to the misleading comforts of
nestling in the warm armpits of the system. Every stage of
livelihood is contaminated.
The plan to create a dead brain in the slave was set in
motion in 1555 and the effect was designed to be selfperpetuating throughout each commencing generation. It is a
mold, a frame of mind, an idea of mental death and
deterioration that permeates the Black nation. Currently the
disconnection of Black people and their failure to come to an
agreement on the time and what must be done continues to
mushroom and snowball at the same time. The problems
loom larger and are more widespread than ever in the 90’s.
Distrust, disunity and disagreement is rampant in every
Black community, and worse, in nearly every Black
household. The condition of the Blackwoman is the
strongest manifestation of this defect. Clear warnings have come from the problem-solvers of
our era, and some of the solutions have even been presented
by the problem-makers, but to date they have all been
rejected. Groups are splintered and competitive with each
other (this is also witnessed in the Black family). The
warners who came with clear messages have died, been
killed or systematically ignored, and truth has been reduced
to an interpretation of mere opinion.
It does not matter now how much financial help is given
to the Black nation, or how much is taken away. If the basic
foundation of the Blackman and Blackwoman’s attachment
179
is not repaired the Black nation in America will continue to
self-destruct without additional help or hindrance from
anyone. It is already on a roll. Superior education, business
advancement, sport achievement and marvelous
entertainment is not enough. We must breed a new people
and teach them new ideas.
Blackmen and Blackwomen have a clear choice to make,
a choice to live resulting from change, or a choice to die
from rejecting this truth. This book poses a question of
survival and the individual choices made by the Blackman
and the Blackwoman about this situation will determine
whether or not the Black nation will slowly heal, or continue
to die a shameful, disrespectful, and unnatural death.
There is no point in being embarassed about the facts
presented in this text because they simply outline the current
effect of four centuries of slavery on the minds and actions
of Black people - all of the Black people, including the
Colored, the Negro, the Blacks, the Afro-American and the
new title: African-American. It is still not settled about who
we are. And superficial unity based on racial identification is
not adequate for progress anyway. The sheer ignorance and
mimicry that Blacks practice is recognized and known
throughout the civilized world. The pitiful state of condition
of the Blackman, the Blackwoman and the Black child is not
hidden, certainly not from the people who caused it to be this
way. Remember: the Black nation is not responsible for
being enslaved, however, we are responsible for our
freedom. And until Black couples make a conscious decision
to practice the behavioral modification required to change the
slavery thought patterns, and combat the practices that
perpetuate failure and dependency, the Black family will
continue on the downhill rollercoaster to hell and extinction it
is currently riding. We can no longer look to the people who
put us in this condition to provide us a way out of it. We
must do this ourselves. We must plan sanely for our futures.
So make no mistake about it, this writing is a call for the
blood. The special blood of the seed of the original
Blackmen who have within their genes the knowledge of
great legacies of progress and eternal peace. Every Blackman
will not heed this call. For some of them it is already too late
- they have been emotionally destroyed. Their problems are
only symptoms of the causes - which are detailed in this
180
book. This writing is also a call to the Blackman to confront
and conquer the terror filled veins flowing throughout the
Blackwoman’s body, and it is a platform for the Blackman
to learn what he must do to survive. No Blackman is exempt
from these instructions. Repeat: No Blackman is exempt
from this information. He must elevate himself to the
occasion. Rise Blackman, and take your rightful place as
ruler of the universe and everything in it. Including the
Blackwoman.
Something About The Author
y Maurice Brian Henderson
I first met Shahrazad Ali back in 1985 at one of her
infamous “Get Off That Hog” lectures in Atlanta. She was
promoting her other book “How Not To Eat Pork (or life
without the pig)” - a wildly controversial but splendid title.
Her intro mentioned that she was a freelance writer
specializing in independent study about social and ethnic
issues. My initial reaction to her was almost one of
overwhelment when I saw this flashy little bolt of thunder
exuding such powerful energy. She approached her audience
with a fervor, gesturing, pointing, reeling like a flaming ball
of light. She gave a descriptive and fascinating presentation
on her case against pork. I got convinced. Her talk rang with
such authority and clarity that the entire audience was
mesmerized with her topic and the only sound heard in that
room for the next hour or so was Shahrazad’s voice. We met
shortly after when she autographed my copy of her book and
we became no less than instant friends. I later learned she
had managed to single handedly reach over 20 million people
informing them about the dangers of eating pork and
possibly saved many lives. I’m a writer too so I was
impressed.
Today we’re both in Philadelphia, both still writing and
still communicating with each other. She calls periodically to
bring me up to date on her writing career, informs me about
her family, her businesses and her volunteer community
work. She’s a dedicated woman and mother. She also keeps
182
me abreast of her new ideas, and when Shahrazad tells you
about a new idea she presents it with such enthusiasm and
excitement that she leaves you literally dying to do whatever
she’s talking about. This is precisely what happened when
she called to tell me about her latest project “The Blackman’s
Guide...” I knew immediately she had another hit.
At that time my own love life reeked of pretension and
artificiality - in other words she was lying and I was
pretending she wasn’t. So I begged Shahrazad to let me take
a peek at her manuscript. She said no! The suspense was
killing me, I couldn’t forget the title. When she finally let me
read it I was captivated. Each page stretched me out, rolled
me over and slapped my face. Brothers, this book contains
the most body slamming information we’ve ever received
from a woman. And Shahrazad is a woman we can trust to
tell us the real deal. She’s not just a fantastic writer, she’s an
educator, a speaker and a self-appointed social scientist. One
thing is for sure, she loves Black people and works hard to
help the Black man out of his dilemmas - especially the one
we’re in with the Black woman, and more importantly she
wants her Black sisters to be happy.
Throughout 1990 Shahrazad plans to tour the U.S.
promoting the “Blackman’s Guide To Understanding The
Blackwoman.” She’s an excellent media personality,
attractive, articulate and entertaining. We can look forward to
an exciting informative year. Part of her tour will feature
what she calls “Black Relationship Seminars” and during
these sessions she’s going to answer our questions and help
point us in the right direction. Shahrazad offers a completely
new view that supports the rights of the Black man to put the
Black woman in her place.
This is a boss idea that’s never been tried before. But
that’s just like Shahrazad, she’s a trailblazer, always forging
ahead, a brave little stick of dynamite that can always be
counted on to bring us the hottest and most explosive
subjects. She craftily tosses them into the ring for
discussion. I hope the brothers who read this book will
thank her for bringing us this life-saving message wrought
with truth and love about our women. Help is no longer on
the way. It has arrived.
183
Publisher’ s Note:
Maurice Brian Henderson is a creative writing instructor
at Temple University in Philadelphia. He recently became a
fellow of the World Literary Academy in Cambridge
England which permits him to do research on Afro-American
art and literature. He also was awarded the Van Pelt College
House Fellow from the University of Pennsylvania. He
graduated from Penn’s Publishing Institute in 1987. He is
currently authoring a book titled “Black Greek-Letteréd
Organizations: A Lesson In African-American Egyptology”
scheduled to be released soon. Maurice is a widely traveled
poet and playwright and has appeared off-Broadway starring
in his own work.
